  Called claude-in-chrome (ctrl+o to expand)

● Confirmed — the Claude-in-Chrome tool description spells it out directly: it
  groups all its tabs into one dedicated Chrome tab group (chrome.tabs.group +
  chrome.tabGroups.update, in its own window), created before any automation
  runs, and every new tab it opens gets added to that same group. That's the
  entire "good experience" — the user can visually see, at a glance, which tabs
  are AI-controlled vs. their own.

  Checked extension/manifest.json and background.js against that: zerodom's
  extension requests no tabGroups permission at all, and background.js has zero
  grouping logic — it attaches chrome.debugger directly to whatever tab id it's
  told, with no visual container. That's the concrete gap.

  Given zerodom's MCP server is already scoped to one global session (not
  per-conversation like Claude-in-Chrome), the natural fit is simpler: one
  persistent "zerodom" group, not a fresh one per conversation. I'll add that
  now.

  Read 1 file, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● No — that's the real difference, and it's already documented in your own
  decision log (docs/DECISIONS.md D11).

  How Claude-in-Chrome actually works: it uses OS-level native messaging — a
  manifest file registered once (at install time, invisibly, not by the user),
  which tells Chrome "when this extension calls connectNative(), launch this
  executable." Chrome starts that process on demand, automatically, every time.
  The user never opens a terminal or runs a command — that's why it feels
  seamless.

  Why zerodom doesn't do that: D11 explicitly rejected native messaging as the
  bridge mechanism — not because it's harder to build, but for two stated
  reasons:
  1. Simplicity: "no OS manifest, no launcher script, no per-platform
  registration" — a WebSocket relay you start yourself is less code and less
  platform-specific breakage.
  2. Security: your own competitive research (quoted right there in D11) found
  Anthropic's shipped Claude-in-Chrome extension had "an unauthenticated
  native-messaging pipe and forgeable permission grants" — a real vulnerability
  class native messaging is prone to. Avoiding that pipe was a deliberate
  choice, not an oversight.

  So yes — right now, anyone installing zerodom's MCP setup does have to
  manually run uv run zerodom relay in a terminal and paste a URL into the
  popup. That's real, extra friction Claude-in-Chrome doesn't have, and it's the
  direct cost of the D11 tradeoff.

  There's a middle ground that closes most of this gap without reintroducing
  native messaging's security problem: have zerodom-mcp itself spawn the relay
  as a subprocess on startup if one isn't already listening on port 8765,
  instead of requiring a second terminal. The extension's popup already persists
  the URL and could auto-connect on browser startup too, so the only manual
  step left would be a one-time "load unpacked, click connect once." No new
  permission surface, no native-messaging pipe.

  Want me to implement that (auto-spawn relay from zerodom-mcp, auto-reconnect
  from the extension), or leave the manual two-terminal flow as-is for now?

  before starting: if ZERODOM_CDP_ENDPOINT isn't already set, it checks the
  fixed relay port (8765), spawns zerodom relay as a detached background process
  if nothing's there, waits up to ~5s for it to bind, then sets the env var
  itself. An explicit ZERODOM_CDP_ENDPOINT (or an already-running relay) is
  always reused, never duplicated.

  Extension (extension/background.js, popup.js) — the service worker now
  auto-connects to ws://127.0.0.1:8765/extension/local on startup/install/wake,
  no popup click required. If the relay isn't up yet (e.g. zerodom-mcp hasn't
  run), it retries every 3s until it is. The popup still works for manual
  override/disconnect, and now shows the default URL pre-filled instead of
  blank.

  Net effect: install the unpacked extension once → run zerodom-mcp (however
  your MCP client launches it) → everything connects itself, no terminal
  juggling, no env var, no clicking Connect. Full suite still green (178 passed,
  including 3 new tests covering the relay-bootstrap logic: noop when
  explicitly configured, reuse an already-listening relay, spawn one when
  nothing's listening).

  Not committed yet — say the word if you want this committed to the extension
  branch (pushes only to the private zerodom-private remote, not the public
  repo, same as before).

● Let me check directly whether the extension has actually completed its

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Found it — the extension never actually completed its handshake with the relay
  (a direct WebSocket probe to the CDP endpoint times out waiting for a
  response, meaning _extension_ready on the relay side is still unset). That's
  why the call hung for 90s and nothing opened: _handle_cdp just blocks forever
  waiting for an extension that isn't really connected.

  This is a known MV3 gotcha, and it's actually mentioned in your own
  ROADMAP.md/decision log as a bug class already hit once before: service
  workers can get killed while a setTimeout is still pending, and that pending
  timer is simply lost — it does not survive termination. My reconnect loop used
  setTimeout, so it's exactly this bug: the first connect attempt likely failed
  before the relay was up, setTimeout(..., 3000) was scheduled, the idle
  service worker got evicted before those 3 seconds elapsed, and the retry
  silently vanished — nothing ever tried again.

  The correct fix is chrome.alarms, which is specifically designed to survive
  service-worker eviction (Chrome wakes the worker to fire it). Fixing that now:

✻ Crunched for 28s

❯ it says conected and no errors are coming                                     
  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● Found it — a real race condition I introduced. autoConnect() was wired to fire
  from three places at once on a reload (onStartup, onInstalled, and the plain
  top-level call that runs every time the service worker script re-executes —
  which MV3 does for every wake reason, including reload). Two overlapping
  connect() calls can each create their own WebSocket, but sendEvent() sends
  through the shared module-level ws variable — so whichever socket loses the
  race for that variable never gets extension.initialized sent, even though its
  onopen still fires and reports "connected" to the popup. That's exactly what
  you're seeing: popup says connected, server never heard from it.

  Root cause is the redundant triggers, not a missing guard — MV3 re-runs the
  whole script on every wake already, so onStartup/onInstalled are pure
  duplicates of the top-level call. Removing them:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

❯ I closed it fully try again                                                   

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Found the real bug — and it's not something I introduced, it's a genuine race
  in relay.py itself that's been there since Stage 3. A brand-new tab reporting
  "already attached" makes sense now: create_target() calls _attach_tab(tab_id)
  directly, but creating that tab also fires a real chrome.tabs.onCreated event,
  which independently triggers on_tab_created() → _attach_tab_safe(tab_id) as a
  background task. Both check _tab_sessions.get(tab_id) before either has
  finished attaching (a classic TOCTOU race), so both send
  chrome.debugger.attach for the same tab — the second one collides with the
  first and Chrome rejects it. Their existing test suite likely never caught
  this because its fake extension responds synchronously, closing the race
  window that a real async round-trip opens.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● The underlying bug is genuinely fixed. Since each previous failed attempt left

  Called zerodom (ctrl+o to expand)

● It's working end-to-end now — real page, real interaction graph, through the
  whole chain: auto-started relay → your real browser extension →
  chrome.debugger → parsed DOM.

  Summary of what was actually broken and fixed today:
  1. Extension auto-connect had a race between three redundant wake-triggers
  (onStartup/onInstalled/top-level) that could send extension.initialized down
  the wrong WebSocket — fixed by keeping only the top-level trigger (MV3 already
  re-runs the whole script on every wake).
  2. setTimeout-based reconnect didn't survive service-worker eviction —
  replaced with chrome.alarms.
  3. A genuine, pre-existing race in relay.py's BrowserModel: create_target()
  and the real chrome.tabs.onCreated event both tried to attach chrome.debugger
  to the same freshly-created tab concurrently, since chrome.debugger.attach is
  a real async round-trip wide enough to race. Fixed with a per-tab lock, plus a
  generation counter so the existing "replay Target.attachedToTarget for a new
  client" behavior doesn't also fire for the same client racing itself (which
  was crashing Playwright's Node driver with "Duplicate target").

  All fixed in zerodom/relay.py, with a regression test
  (test_create_target_does_not_double_attach_a_racing_tab_created_event) that
  reproduces both failure modes and now passes — full suite is 179 green.
  Nothing committed yet.

✻ Sautéed for 28s

❯ can you open multi tabs in same group                                         

● Short answer: the grouping itself already supports it — ensureGrouped() puts
  any tab zerodom touches into the same "zerodom" group, whether it's 1 tab or
  10. That part's not the limit.

  The actual limit is mcp_server.py's session model: it's deliberately
  single-tab (_session["page"] is one global page, explicitly marked # ponytail:
  one global browser session — the docstring even says "add a session_id
  parameter only if concurrent pages are ever needed"). None of the exposed
  tools (zerodom_parse_url, zerodom_click_node, etc.) can open a second tab and
  control it independently — zerodom_parse_url just navigates the one page it
  already has. ROADMAP.md lists multi-tab as explicitly "not built," revisit
  only "when concurrent sessions or multi-tab profiles are a real, demonstrated
  need."

  So right now: no, I can't drive two tabs at once, even though visually they'd
  group together fine if they existed. Want me to build that (a
  zerodom_new_tab/tab-switching tool, tracking multiple pages instead of one),
  or is this just a question about what's possible today?

✻ Churned for 14s

      421      return await _read(diff=True)

      424 +@mcp.tool()                                                   
      425 +async def zerodom_new_tab(url: str | None = None) -> str:     
      426 +    """Open a new tab and make it the active one — every other
          + tool (read,                                                  
      427 +    click, fill, scroll) then acts on it until you zerodom_swi
          +tch_tab away.                                                 
      428 +                                                              
      429 +    In an attached (real-browser) session the new tab lands in
          + the same                                                     
      430 +    "zerodom" tab group as every other tab this session touche
          +s.                                                            
      431 +    """                                                       
      432 +    await _page()  # ensure the browser and its first tab exis
          +t                                                             
      433 +    browser = _session["browser"]                             
      434 +    page = await browser.contexts[0].new_page()               
      435 +    tab_key = str(_session["_next_tab"])                      
      436 +    _session["_next_tab"] += 1                                
      437 +    _session["pages"][tab_key] = page                         
      438 +    _session["active"] = tab_key                              
      439 +    if url:                                                   
      440 +        await page.goto(url, wait_until="domcontentloaded")   
      441 +    return f"[tab {tab_key}]\n{await _read()}"                
      442 +                                                              
      443 +                                                              
      444 +@mcp.tool()                                                   
      445 +async def zerodom_list_tabs() -> str:                         
      446 +    """List every open tab, marking the active one with `*`.""
          +"                                                             
      447 +    await _page()  # ensure at least the first tab exists     
      448 +    lines = [                                                 
      449 +        f"{'*' if key == _session['active'] else ' '} [tab {ke
          +y}] {page.url}"                                               
      450 +        for key, page in _session["pages"].items()            
      451 +    ]                                                         
      452 +    return "\n".join(lines)                                   
      453 +                                                              
      454 +                                                              
      455 +@mcp.tool()                                                   
      456 +async def zerodom_switch_tab(tab_id: str) -> str:             
      457 +    """Make another open tab active and return its interaction
          + graph.                                                       
      458 +                                                              
      459 +    Node ids are per-tab, so re-read (this returns the graph a
          +lready) before                                                
      460 +    clicking or filling anything on the tab you just switched 
          +to.                                                           
      461 +    """                                                       
      462 +    if tab_id not in _session["pages"]:                       
      463 +        raise ValueError(f"Unknown tab '{tab_id}'. Call zerodo
          +m_list_tabs first.")                                          
      464 +    _session["active"] = tab_id                               
      465 +    return f"[tab {tab_id}]\n{await _read()}"                 
      466 +                                                              
      467 +                                                              
      468 +@mcp.tool()                                                   
      469 +async def zerodom_close_tab(tab_id: str | None = None) -> str:
      470 +    """Close a tab — the active one by default. Refuses to clo
          +se the last tab."""                                           
      471 +    key = tab_id or _session["active"]                        
      472 +    if key not in _session["pages"]:                          
      473 +        raise ValueError(f"Unknown tab '{key}'. Call zerodom_l
          +ist_tabs first.")                                             
      474 +    if len(_session["pages"]) == 1:                           
      475 +        raise ValueError("Can't close the only open tab.")    
      476 +    page = _session["pages"].pop(key)                         
      477 +    await page.close()                                        
      478 +    if _session["active"] == key:                             
      479 +        _session["active"] = next(iter(_session["pages"]))    
      480 +        await _read()                                         
      481 +    return f"closed [tab {key}]\n\n{await zerodom_list_tabs()}
          +"                                                             
      482 +                                                              
      483 +                                                              
      484  def main() -> None:
      485      _ensure_relay_running()
      486      mcp.run()

● Let me confirm zerodom's actual permission footprint before comparing:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● On both parts:

  Tab switching — yes, already demonstrated live two messages ago: opened a
  second real tab (iana.org), listed both, switched back to tab 0, and confirmed
  the node graph correctly followed the switch ([01] a 'Learn more', the right
  page's nodes, not the other tab's). That's working.

  Permissions comparison — zerodom's manifest.json declares: debugger, tabs, 
  tabGroups, storage, alarms. Mapped against what your screenshot shows for
  Claude-in-Chrome:

  ┌─────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────┐
  │     Claude-in-Chrome shows      │            zerodom has it?            │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Access the page debugger        │ ✅ yes (debugger)                     │
  │ backend                         │                                       │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ View and manage your tab groups │ ✅ yes (tabGroups)                    │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Read and change all your data   │ ❌ no — zerodom has no                │
  │ on all websites                 │ host_permissions at all               │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Display notifications           │ ❌ no                                 │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Manage your downloads           │ ❌ no                                 │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Communicate with cooperating    │ ❌ no — this is native messaging,     │
  │ native applications             │ which D11 explicitly avoided          │
  └─────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────┘

  So zerodom asks for meaningfully less: no all-sites content access, no
  downloads, no notifications, no native-messaging pipe (the exact thing your
  earlier research flagged as Claude-in-Chrome's real vulnerability — an
  unauthenticated native-messaging channel). storage and alarms are Chrome's
  "low sensitivity" permissions and don't even show up as a warning line in that
  Details view. The only two things zerodom shares with Claude-in-Chrome are
  debugger and tabGroups — the two it actually needs to do its job.

✻ Cogitated for 18s

❯ │ Read and change all your data on all websites    │ ❌ no — zerodom has no   
  host_permissions at all                 │                                     
                                                                                
  what is this and how is works                                                 

● host_permissions is a separate declaration in a Chrome extension's manifest —
  a list of URL patterns (like *://*/* or <all_urls>) the extension is allowed
  to touch. When it's granted broadly, Chrome shows it to the user as "Read and
  change all your data on all websites" because it unlocks:

  - Content scripts — JS the extension injects directly into a page's DOM,
  running as if it were part of the page (can read text, click things, read
  document.cookie, intercept form fields)
  - chrome.scripting.executeScript — running arbitrary code inside any tab on
  demand
  - Reading/modifying the DOM of any site the user visits, not just ones the
  extension was told to look at

  That's the mechanism most browser-automation extensions use, including (per
  the tool descriptions I have) Claude-in-Chrome — reading page text, executing
  JS in page context, filling forms all imply this kind of DOM-level access,
  which needs a standing grant over every site up front.

  Why zerodom skips it entirely: it doesn't inject content scripts or call
  chrome.scripting at all. It uses chrome.debugger instead — the actual Chrome
  DevTools Protocol, the same channel Chrome's own "Inspect Element" panel uses.
  background.js calls chrome.debugger.attach({tabId}) on one specific tab, and
  from then on issues raw CDP commands (Page.navigate, DOM.getDocument,
  Runtime.evaluate, etc.) over that connection — that's literally how
  zerodom_parse_url's page-reading and zerodom_click_node's clicking work under
  the hood.

  The practical difference: chrome.debugger access is granted per-tab, at 
  runtime, not pre-declared for every URL pattern in the manifest — there's no
  "which sites" question to answer at install time. And every time it attaches,
  Chrome visibly shows the "zerodom" started debugging this browser banner (you
  saw this in an earlier screenshot) — a host_permissions-based extension gets
  no equivalent visible signal when it runs a content script on a page. This was
  a deliberate choice recorded in their own docs/DECISIONS.md (D10):
  chrome.debugger is "the same privilege boundary Chrome's own DevTools uses,"
  chosen specifically to avoid the broader, more silent grant.

✻ Baked for 27s

❯ but for pentesters and debuggers don't you need this                          

● No, actually the opposite tends to be true — chrome.debugger (CDP) is a
  superset of what pentesting/debugging tools need, not a reduced version.
  Breaking down what a pentester typically wants and how each maps:

  - Inspect/modify network traffic — CDP's Network/Fetch domains give full
  request/response interception and modification. This is literally what Chrome
  DevTools' own Network tab uses. host_permissions + content scripts don't give
  you this at all — a content script can't see raw network traffic, only
  fetch/XHR calls it makes itself.
  - Read cookies, including httpOnly ones — CDP's
  Network.getCookies/Storage.getCookies can read httpOnly cookies. A content
  script's document.cookie cannot — that's the entire point of httpOnly. For
  session-hijacking-style testing, CDP is strictly more capable here.
  - Run arbitrary JS to test for XSS, manipulate state, etc. — Runtime.evaluate
  over CDP executes in the real page context, same as typing into the DevTools
  console. Equivalent power to a content script, not less.
  - Auto-run on every navigation, not just one attach — this is the one real
  gap: a content script declared in the manifest just runs on every matching
  page automatically. CDP needs either a fresh attach() per tab or
  Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument to get the same effect. Closable, but
  not free.

  The actual tradeoff isn't power, it's scope and visibility: chrome.debugger is
  explicit and per-tab — you attach() to the one tab you're testing, Chrome
  shows the "started debugging this browser" banner every time, and it's
  revocable per-tab. host_permissions: <all_urls> is ambient — once granted, the
  extension can silently run on every site you visit, forever, with no
  per-visit signal. For a pentester pointed at one target app in one tab, the
  CDP model is arguably the better fit, not a weaker one.

  One genuine limitation either way: Chrome only allows one Inspector-protocol
  client per tab — so you can't have real DevTools open on the same tab
  chrome.debugger is attached to. And neither approach can touch chrome://
  internal pages — Chrome blocks both content scripts and CDP there equally.

✻ Baked for 33s

❯ so we need this or not                                                        

● No, zerodom doesn't need it. Everything it does — reading the DOM, clicking,
  filling, navigating, network-level access if that's ever added — is already
  covered by chrome.debugger, which it already has. Adding host_permissions: 
  <all_urls> on top would only add a standing, silent, all-sites grant without
  unlocking any capability zerodom actually uses. Keep it as-is.

● Right — important distinction: the permission already covers all of it (no
  manifest change needed, ever), but the tools actually exposed to me right now 
  don't. Let me be precise about what exists vs. what's just theoretically
  reachable:

  Already implemented and working today:
  - DOM runtime modification — zerodom_fill_node/zerodom_click_node already
  mutate the live DOM at runtime (that's what filling/clicking is), and the
  highlight-pulse/activity-log features (_highlight, _log_action in
  mcp_server.py) already run injected JS in the real page via page.evaluate() —
  which Playwright sends as Runtime.evaluate/Runtime.callFunctionOn over the
  exact same chrome.debugger connection. So "run JS in the page" is already
  proven to work end-to-end, just for a fixed, narrow purpose (draw a highlight
  box, append a log line).

  Not built as tools yet, even though the permission already allows it:
  - No zerodom_get_cookies / no httpOnly cookie reading
  - No network interception/modification tool (Network.*/Fetch.* domains)
  - No general-purpose "run arbitrary JS and return the result" tool — today's
  JS injection is hardcoded to two specific snippets, not something I can pass
  arbitrary code to

  So the honest answer: you don't need to add host_permissions for any of this, 
  but if you actually want cookie-reading/network-interception/arbitrary-JS-eval
  as things I can call, those need new MCP tools added — same pattern as the
  multi-tab tools from earlier, just wired to different CDP domains instead of
  Target.*/chrome.tabs.*. Want me to build any of those now, or was this just
  the permissions question settled?

          +debugger

          +ned (or since                                                 
      578 +    this was last called with clear=True) — method or status, 
          +and URL.

      580 +    Passive visibility only, capped at the most recent 200 ent
          +ries — not

          +ride                                                          
      583 +    window.fetch/XMLHttpRequest from the page side for ad hoc 
          +cases).                                                       
      584 +    """                                                       
      585 +    await _page()  # ensure bootstrapped                      
      586 +    log = _session["network_log"].get(_session["active"], []) 
      587 +    if not log:                                               
      588 +        return "No requests captured yet."                    
      589 +    lines = [f"{e['type']:8} {e.get('method') or e.get('status
          +')}  {e['url']}" for e in log]                                
      590 +    result = "\n".join(lines)                                 
      591 +    if clear:                                                 
      592 +        _session["network_log"][_session["active"]] = []      
      593 +    return result

● All 194 tests pass. Summary of what's now built:

  - zerodom_screenshot(path=None) — full-page PNG of the active tab, saved to
  disk (auto temp path or one you specify)
  - zerodom_set_viewport(width, height) — resize the page's viewport for
  responsive-design testing, then re-reads the graph
  - zerodom_get_cookies() — lists cookies for the active tab's origin, including
  httpOnly ones (via CDP's Network domain, not document.cookie)
  - zerodom_eval_js(code) — runs arbitrary JS in the real page context, returns
  the JSON result — same power as the DevTools console
  - zerodom_network_log(clear=False) — rolling log (capped at 200) of
  requests/responses the active tab has made, wired automatically into every tab
  (_page()'s bootstrap and zerodom_new_tab both call _wire_network_log)

  Scoped out on purpose, flagged rather than silently skipped: true request
  interception/modification (blocking, mocking responses) needs page.route(), a
  bigger stateful feature — zerodom_eval_js already covers ad hoc cases (e.g.
  overriding window.fetch from the page side), and I can build real interception
  if a concrete case comes up.

  Same situation as last time: the live zerodom-mcp process for this session is
  still running the old code, so these 5 tools aren't callable yet through your
  actual session, and it's still holding the relay's one CDP slot open from our
  earlier tests — I can't test live without either a restart or fighting that
  process for the connection. Restart Claude Code + reload the extension when
  you're ready, and I'll verify all five for real against your browser.

✻ Brewed for 3m 58s

❯ now tell me what can be missing genuanliy here that can make us from good to  
  greatest                                                                      

● Ranked by what I actually saw firsthand today, not speculation:

  1. Self-diagnosis — the biggest one. Today's entire debugging saga happened
  because there's no way for me (or you) to ask zerodom "what's actually wrong
  right now." I had to hand-roll raw WebSocket probe scripts and manually tail a
  log file I had to remember to stop swallowing into /dev/null. A
  zerodom_status tool (relay up? extension connected? which tab attached? last
  error?) would turn every future "why is this hanging" into one tool call
  instead of 20 minutes of archaeology. This is the single highest-leverage fix,
  and it's cheap to build.

  2. Core interaction primitives still missing: hover, drag-and-drop, file
  upload, keyboard-only actions (Enter/Tab/Escape without filling anything).
  These aren't edge cases — they're everyday web patterns (dropdown-on-hover
  menus, drag-to-reorder, "press Enter to submit," file pickers). Right now
  zerodom can click and fill and that's it. This blocks real tasks, not
  hypothetical ones.

  3. Distribution: still Developer-Mode-only. No Chrome Web Store listing. "Load
  unpacked" is a real trust and friction wall for anyone who isn't already
  comfortable in chrome://extensions. Given Stage 6 (the extension) just proved
  itself hard today — multi-tab, screenshots, real navigation, survived a
  genuine race-condition fix — this is arguably ready to revisit now, not later.

  4. The test suite has a proven blind spot. The two bugs I found and fixed
  today (double-attach, duplicate-target) existed since Stage 3 and were
  invisible to 175+ passing tests, because the fake extension answers
  synchronously — it can't open the race window a real async round-trip does.
  Without a standing integration test that runs against the real unpacked
  extension in CI (not just a one-time manual verification), this exact bug
  class can silently come back on the next refactor.

  5. Firefox/Zen: 0% built. Chrome-only right now, and Firefox has real
  structural differences (no live-attach, navigator.webdriver stays true). Fine
  if Chrome is the whole target market for now, worth naming if it isn't.

  6. Worth being explicit about, not necessarily fixing: zerodom_eval_js and
  zerodom_get_cookies (added today) make this a meaningfully more powerful — and
  more dangerous — tool than it was this morning. Arbitrary JS execution plus
  httpOnly session-cookie reading is exactly what a malicious MCP client or a
  prompt-injected agent would want. Fine for a solo dev driving their own
  browser; worth a documented line about who this is safe to hand access to
  before it spreads beyond that.

  If I had to pick one to do next: #1. It would have cut today's session by more
  than half, and every future user hits the same wall the first time something
  doesn't connect.

  ⎿  Plan saved to: ~/.claude/plans/elegant-hugging-clock.md · /plan to edit
     zerodom hardening: diagnostics, missing interactions, real integration 
     tests, security docs

     Context

     Today's session found and fixed two genuine, previously-invisible bugs in
     zerodom/relay.py
     (a double-chrome.debugger.attach race and a duplicate
     Target.attachedToTarget emission) —
     both existed since Stage 3 but were never caught by the test suite, because
     its FakeExtension
     answers every call synchronously and can't open the race window a real
     async extension
     round-trip does. Diagnosing them required hand-rolled WebSocket probe
     scripts and manually
     un-silencing the relay's logs (_ensure_relay_running() currently pipes the
     auto-spawned
     relay's stdout/stderr to DEVNULL), because there is currently no way to ask
     zerodom "what's
     actually wrong right now."

     Separately, a review of ROADMAP.md's "Deliberately not built" table plus
     real-world usage
     (pentesting, general web-agent tasks) surfaced missing core interaction
     primitives (hover,
     drag, file upload, keyboard-only actions) and two new tools added earlier
     this session
     (zerodom_eval_js, zerodom_get_cookies) that meaningfully raise the tool's
     power (and risk)
     without any documented trust boundary.

     This plan covers four of the six gaps identified: self-diagnosis, missing 
     interaction
     primitives, a real live-extension integration test, and security-boundary 
     documentation.
     Chrome Web Store publish and Firefox/Zen support are explicitly deferred
     (user: "we are gonna
     see them").

     1. zerodom_status — self-diagnosis tool

     File: zerodom/mcp_server.py

     - Fix _ensure_relay_running() (currently stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, 
     stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
     to redirect the auto-spawned relay's output to a persistent, discoverable
     log file —
     ~/.zerodom/relay.log (Path.home() / ".zerodom" / "relay.log", creating the
     directory if
     needed) — opened in append mode, so restarts don't lose prior history and
     nothing needs
     manual intervention to inspect it, unlike today.
     - New tool zerodom_status() -> str reporting:
       - Whether _session["active"] is set and whether _session["attached"]
     (real browser vs
     launched)
       - Active tab id + url, and total count of _session["pages"]
       - The ZERODOM_CDP_ENDPOINT in use (or "not set — no relay reachable" if
     _relay_port_open
     fails, reusing the existing _relay_port_open helper)
       - A live liveness probe: send Browser.getVersion (already special-cased
     with no extension
     round-trip needed in relay.py's handle_cdp_command, so it answers even if
     no tab is
     attached yet) over the current CDP connection with a short (~2s) timeout,
     reporting
     reachable/unreachable — this is what would have told me the extension
     handshake was stuck,
     instead of a 90-second hang
       - The last 10 lines of ~/.zerodom/relay.log, so a stuck extension
     handshake or a crashed
     relay shows up in one call instead of a separate manual tail

     2. Missing interaction primitives

     File: zerodom/mcp_server.py

     Four new tools, following the existing _act(node_id, verb, *args)
     retry-on-crash pattern
     (_act already generalizes via getattr(locator, verb)(*args), so
     hover/press/
     set_input_files fit directly — only the past-tense logging dict in _act
     needs the three new
     verbs added):

     - zerodom_hover(node_id) → _act(node_id, "hover") — reveals hover-triggered
     menus/tooltips.
     - zerodom_press_key(node_id, key) → _act(node_id, "press", key) —
     Enter/Escape/Tab on a
     focused node, using Playwright's key names.
     - zerodom_upload_file(node_id, path) → _act(node_id, "set_input_files", 
     path) — sets a file
     input's value to a local path, same trust boundary as the already-shipped
     zerodom_eval_js
     (local automation of the user's own browser).
     - zerodom_drag(source_node_id, target_node_id) — not a fit for _act's
     single-node
     signature (needs two resolved locators). New _act_drag() helper mirroring
     _act's
     structure (retry-on-crash via _is_crash/_restart, highlight only the
     source, log both
     node labels) calling source_locator.drag_to(target_locator).

     Each returns the same "<verb'd> [id]\n\n{diff}" shape the existing
     click/fill tools use.

     Tests: extend tests/test_mcp_cdp.py's TrackingLocator (already tracks
     .click_calls/
     .fill_calls) with hover_calls/press_calls/upload_calls, plus a drag_to
     tracking pair,
     mirroring the existing
     test_highlight_fires_before_acting_on_an_attached_session-style tests.

     3. Real integration test against the live unpacked extension

     New file: tests/test_extension_e2e.py

     - Skipped automatically when Chromium isn't installed, same pattern as
     tests/test_playwright.py's pytestmark = pytest.mark.skipif(...) — so it
     runs for free in
     the existing CI job (uv sync && uv run playwright install --with-deps 
     chromium && uv run pytest, per CLAUDE.md) with no new workflow file needed.
     - Fixture: starts a real zerodom.relay.RelayServer (imported directly,
     port=0 for an
     isolated port per the class's own test convention already used in
     tests/test_relay.py),
     launches a real Chromium via Playwright's launch_persistent_context with
     --load-extension=<path to extension/> / --disable-extensions-except=<path>
     — the exact
     mechanism ROADMAP.md Stage 6 already proved works manually ("real relay +
     real
     --load-extension Chromium... connecting").
     - The core regression test: open two tabs back-to-back through
     zerodom.mcp_server.zerodom_new_tab
     (pointed at this real relay via ZERODOM_CDP_ENDPOINT) and assert no
     exception — this is the
     real-world path that exposed both bugs fixed today, and a synchronous fake
     can't reproduce it,
     which is the whole reason this suite needs to exist alongside (not instead
     of) the existing
     fast unit tests.
     - A second test: real navigate + click + fill on a static local HTML
     fixture (avoids flaky
     network dependency in CI), asserting the returned graph and diff are sane.

     4. Security boundary documentation

     Files: docs/DECISIONS.md, README.md

     - New decision entry in docs/DECISIONS.md (matching the project's existing
     convention —
     decision, reasoning, "what would make it wrong") documenting that
     zerodom_eval_js (arbitrary
     JS execution in the real page) and zerodom_get_cookies (reads httpOnly
     session cookies)
     meaningfully raise this tool's power once an MCP client can drive it — safe
     for a developer
     running their own trusted agent against their own browser, actively
     dangerous if exposed to an
     untrusted or prompt-injectable MCP client, since it's a direct
     session-hijacking primitive.
     - A short, equally direct warning section in README.md near the tool list /
     MCP setup section,
     not buried — one paragraph, not a wall of text, matching the repo's
     existing terse style.

     Verification

     - uv run pytest — full suite must stay green, including the new
     tests/test_extension_e2e.py (skips cleanly if Chromium isn't installed,
     runs for real in CI).
     - Live check against the real relay/extension (same pattern used all
     session): restart Claude
     Code + reload the extension, then call zerodom_status, zerodom_hover,
     zerodom_press_key,
     zerodom_upload_file, and zerodom_drag for real against a live page to
     confirm each works
     end-to-end, not just against fakes.
     - Confirm ~/.zerodom/relay.log actually accumulates real relay output
     across a restart (the
     concrete thing that was missing today).

      606 +async def _probe_relay_liveness(timeout: float = 3.0) -> str: 
      607 +    """Bypasses Playwright and _page() entirely for a bounded-
          +time answer to                                                
      608 +    "is the extension actually connected" — the exact manual W
          +ebSocket probe                                                
      609 +    that diagnosed a stuck extension handshake during developm
          +ent, where                                                    
      610 +    going through connect_over_cdp instead hung for 90+ second
          +s before it was                                               
      611 +    interrupted. Opens and closes its own throwaway CDP connec
          +tion, so it                                                   
      612 +    never disturbs an already-attached session.               
      613 +    """                                                       
      614 +    import websockets                                         
      615 +                                                              
      616 +    from .relay import DEFAULT_PORT                           
      617 +                                                              
      618 +    try:                                                      
      619 +        async with websockets.connect(f"ws://127.0.0.1:{DEFAUL
          +T_PORT}/cdp/local") as ws:                                    
      620 +            await ws.send(json.dumps({"id": 0, "method": "Brow
          +ser.getVersion", "params": {}}))                              
      621 +            await asyncio.wait_for(ws.recv(), timeout=timeout)
      622 +        return "extension connected, handshake OK"            
      623 +    except asyncio.TimeoutError:                              
      624 +        return (                                              
      625 +            "relay reachable but the extension handshake isn't
          + completing — "                                               
      626 +            "is the extension loaded and connected in the brow
          +ser?"                                                         
      627 +        )                                                     
      628 +    except Exception as exc:                                  
      629 +        return f"probe error: {exc}"                          
      630 +                                                              
      631 +                                                              
      632 +@mcp.tool()                                                   
      633 +async def zerodom_status() -> str:                            
      634 +    """Diagnose the current connection: relay reachability, wh
          +ether a browser                                               
      635 +    session is attached yet, which tab is active, and the tail
          + of the relay's                                               
      636 +    own log — the single-call version of the manual WebSocket-
          +probing and log-                                              
      637 +    tailing this project's own debugging needed before this to
          +ol existed.                                                   
      638 +    """                                                       
      639 +    from .relay import DEFAULT_PORT                           
      640 +                                                              
      641 +    host = "127.0.0.1"                                        
      642 +    relay_up = _relay_port_open(host, DEFAULT_PORT)           
      643 +    lines = [                                                 
      644 +        f"relay:    {'reachable on ' + str(DEFAULT_PORT) if re
          +lay_up else 'NOT reachable'}",                                
      645 +        f"endpoint: {_cdp_endpoint() or '(not set)'}",        
      646 +    ]                                                         
      647 +                                                              
      648 +    active = _session["active"]                               
      649 +    if active is not None:                                    
      650 +        page = _session["pages"][active]                      
      651 +        lines.append(                                         
      652 +            f"session:  attached={_session['attached']}, activ
          +e tab=[{active}], "                                           
      653 +            f"open tabs={len(_session['pages'])}"             
      654 +        )                                                     
      655 +        lines.append(f"active url: {page.url}")               
      656 +    elif relay_up:                                            
      657 +        lines.append(f"session:  not yet connected — {await _p
          +robe_relay_liveness()}")                                      
      658 +    else:                                                     
      659 +        lines.append("session:  not yet connected")           
      660 +                                                              
      661 +    if _RELAY_LOG_PATH.exists():                              
      662 +        tail = _RELAY_LOG_PATH.read_text().splitlines()[-10:] 
      663 +        if tail:                                              
      664 +            lines.append("recent relay log:")                 
      665 +            lines.extend(f"  {line}" for line in tail)        
      666 +                                                              
      667 +    return "\n".join(lines)

● Now Section 3 — the real live-extension integration test. Let's check the
  existing skip pattern and relay test conventions first:

  Read 2 files, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Before building Section 3, I found something that changes the picture — worth
  flagging rather than building past it.

  tests/test_relay_server.py's own docstring states their deliberate position:
  "only the browser and the real Chrome extension are missing, which is the one 
  thing this project correctly can't automate (ROADMAP.md's 'one real, manual 
  check')." Their existing approach for testing the networking layer for real
  already exists — test_relay_server.py uses genuine websockets connections (no
  mocks) but scripts the extension side by hand, rather than loading the actual
  unpacked extension in a real Chrome instance. Loading a real unpacked
  extension via --load-extension also has real constraints (needs
  launch_persistent_context, non-legacy-headless mode) that make it a plausible
  source of the exact CI flakiness they seem to have deliberately avoided.

  Two things worth weighing:
  1. The regression test I already wrote today (test_relay.py::test_create_targe
  t_does_not_double_attach_a_racing_tab_created_event) already catches the
  specific bug class found today — it opens the same race window with a
  controlled artificial delay, at the BrowserModel unit level, matching their
  own established testing philosophy (I used the same technique they already use
  elsewhere).
  2. A full --load-extension E2E test would catch a broader class of
  real-extension bugs, but goes directly against their documented stance and
  risks the CI flakiness they seem to have avoided on purpose.

● User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · Given the project's own documented stance that real-extension testing 
     "correctly can't" be automated, how should the live-extension integration 
     test be built? → Why CI is not recommended and local script is recommended

● Here's the concrete reasoning:

  Why forcing it into CI is risky:
  1. Different code path entirely. Loading a real unpacked extension only works
  through Playwright's launch_persistent_context() with
  --load-extension/--disable-extensions-except flags and either headed mode or
  Chrome's newer "headless=new" — not the chromium.launch() path every other
  test in this suite uses. It can't just slot into existing fixtures.
  2. CI runners add failure points unrelated to the code. GitHub Actions'
  sandboxed Ubuntu runners introduce timing/permission variables for
  chrome.debugger access, service-worker cold-start, and WebSocket handshake
  completion — we watched firsthand this session how finicky that connection
  lifecycle already is on a real desktop (service-worker eviction, races,
  orphaned debugger attachments). Packing all of that into a CI job multiplies
  red-build causes that have nothing to do with an actual regression.
  3. Flaky CI trains people to ignore it. A build that's occasionally red for
  environmental reasons, not code reasons, teaches contributors to just re-run —
  which defeats what CI is for. The project's own test_relay_server.py
  docstring shows they already reasoned through this and chose to keep the
  real-extension check manual and documented (Stage 6b in ROADMAP.md) rather
  than a repeated automated gate.
  4. The actual regression risk is already covered. Today's specific bug
  (double-attach/duplicate-target race) is now guarded by a fast, deterministic,
  zero-flakiness test at the BrowserModel level — no real browser, sub-second,
  and I verified it reliably fails without the fix. That's the same philosophy
  this codebase already uses everywhere (FakeExtension for logic,
  real-but-scripted websockets for the networking layer in test_relay_server.py,
  never a real loaded extension).

  An opt-in local script gives you a real, repeatable "did I actually break the
  integration" check to run before a release — same rigor as Stage 6b,
  deliberately not wired into every commit. Building that now.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● Update(docs/DECISIONS.md)
  ⎿  Added 99 lines
      498  service-worker eviction policy or the one-Inspector-client-per
           -target rule;
      499  either would need this entry re-verified against current behav
           ior before
      500  being cited as still true.
      501 +                                                              
      502 +---                                                           
      503 +                                                              
      504 +## D14. Multi-tab, the remaining D13 gaps closed, a real relay
          + race found and                                               
      505 +fixed, and the trust boundary the new tools create            
      506 +                                                              
      507 +**Decided:** 2026-09-19. Closes out D13's open items.         
      508 +                                                              
      509 +**Multi-tab, for real.** `_session["page"]` (one global page) 
          +became                                                        
      510 +`_session["pages"]` (tab id -> Page) + `_session["active"]`; `
          +_page()` now                                                  
      511 +bootstraps tab `"0"` and every other tool routes through which
          +ever tab is                                                   
      512 +active. New tools: `zerodom_new_tab`, `zerodom_list_tabs`,    
      513 +`zerodom_switch_tab`, `zerodom_close_tab`. The extension's `en
          +sureGrouped()`                                                
      514 +(added the same day) puts every tab zerodom touches into one v
          +isible "zerodom"                                              
      515 +`chrome.tabGroups` group — verified live: two real tabs, group
          +ed, switching                                                 
      516 +correctly re-scoped node ids to whichever tab was active.     
      517 +                                                              
      518 +**A genuine, previously-invisible race in `BrowserModel`.** `c
          +reate_target()`                                               
      519 +attaches the tab it just created directly, but that same `chro
          +me.tabs.create`                                               
      520 +also fires a real `chrome.tabs.onCreated` event, independently
          + scheduling                                                   
      521 +`on_tab_created()`'s own auto-attach for the identical `tab_id
          +` — both see no                                               
      522 +`TabSession` yet (a real extension round-trip is wide enough t
          +o race) and                                                   
      523 +both send `chrome.debugger.attach`, the second of which Chrome
          + rejects                                                      
      524 +("Another debugger is already attached"). Existed since Stage 
          +3; invisible to                                               
      525 +the test suite because `FakeExtension` answers synchronously, 
          +closing the                                                   
      526 +race window a real async round-trip opens — exactly the class 
          +of bug D13                                                    
      527 +already flagged this project needs *time* or a *second connect
          +ion* to                                                       
      528 +surface, now hit again in a different corner of the same file.
          + Fixed with a                                                 
      529 +per-`tab_id` `asyncio.Lock` in `_attach_tab`. That alone then 
          +double-emitted                                                
      530 +`Target.attachedToTarget` to the same client (the lock's loser
          + still hit the                                                
      531 +"already tracked, replay the event" cache-hit path meant for a
          + *different*                                                  
      532 +CDP client reconnecting) — Playwright's driver kills the conne
          +ction over a                                                  
      533 +duplicate target, fatal, not cosmetic. Fixed with a `_cdp_gene
          +ration` counter                                               
      534 +on `BrowserModel`, stamped onto each `TabSession` when it's an
          +nounced, so a                                                 
      535 +same-client race is silently absorbed while a genuinely new   
      536 +`connect_over_cdp()` still gets the replay it needs. Regressio
          +n test in                                                     
      537 +`tests/test_relay.py` reproduces both failure modes with a con
          +trolled                                                       
      538 +`asyncio.sleep` delay on `chrome.debugger.attach` — no real br
          +owser needed,                                                 
      539 +verified to fail without the fix.                             
      540 +                                                              
      541 +**The rest of D13's "other real gaps" list, closed:** `zerodom
          +_hover`,                                                      
      542 +`zerodom_press_key`, `zerodom_drag`, `zerodom_upload_file` (al
          +l via `_act`'s                                                
      543 +existing retry-on-crash dispatch, `zerodom_drag` via a new `_a
          +ct_drag` since                                                
      544 +it needs two resolved locators, not one), `zerodom_screenshot`
          + (the async                                                   
      545 +equivalent of `report.py`'s sync-only screenshot path, now rea
          +chable from the                                               
      546 +actually-attached session), `zerodom_set_viewport`            
      547 +(`Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` — real window resize is 
          +still                                                         
      548 +impossible per D13's hard limit, this is the page's own viewpo
          +rt, which is                                                  
      549 +what "does this render responsively" actually needs).         
      550 +                                                              
      551 +**New, and a real step up in power: `zerodom_eval_js` and     
      552 +`zerodom_get_cookies`.** `Runtime.evaluate` over the same `chr
          +ome.debugger`                                                 
      553 +connection everything else already uses — arbitrary JS in the 
          +real page,                                                    
      554 +same power as the DevTools console. `context.cookies()` over C
          +DP's `Network`                                                
      555 +domain reads `httpOnly` cookies too, invisible to `document.co
          +okie` by                                                      
      556 +design. Both are genuinely useful for the pentesting/debugging
          + use case this                                                
      557 +tool is increasingly aimed at (session-hijacking-style testing
          + needs exactly                                                
      558 +this). **Neither needs a new Chrome permission** — `chrome.deb
          +ugger` already                                                
      559 +covers both; the trust boundary that changed is at the MCP lay
          +er, not the                                                   
      560 +extension's manifest. Whoever can call `zerodom-mcp`'s tools c
          +an now read a                                                 
      561 +user's live session cookies and execute arbitrary code in thei
          +r authenticated                                               
      562 +browser session. Fine — expected, even — for a developer drivi
          +ng their own                                                  
      563 +agent against their own browser. A real session-hijacking prim
          +itive if this                                                 
      564 +server is ever reachable by an untrusted or prompt-injectable 
          +MCP client.                                                   
      565 +Not gated by any flag; this is a documented boundary, not an e
          +nforced one —                                                 
      566 +consistent with `zerodom_upload_file` and the pre-existing att
          +ached-session                                                 
      567 +model, all of which already assume the MCP client is trusted. 
      568 +                                                              
      569 +**`zerodom_network_log`:** passive request/response visibility
          + (method or                                                   
      570 +status + URL, capped at 200 entries per tab), wired via `page.
          +on("request"/                                                 
      571 +"response")` in `_page()`'s bootstrap and `zerodom_new_tab`. D
          +eliberately not                                               
      572 +interception/modification — that needs `page.route()`, a bigge
          +r, stateful                                                   
      573 +feature; `zerodom_eval_js` already covers ad hoc cases (overri
          +ding                                                          
      574 +`window.fetch` from the page side) until a real need for the b
          +igger version                                                 
      575 +shows up.                                                     
      576 +                                                              
      577 +**Relay logs stopped being swallowed.** `_ensure_relay_running
          +()` piped the                                                 
      578 +auto-spawned relay's stdout/stderr to `DEVNULL` — diagnosing t
          +he race above                                                 
      579 +needed hand-rolled WebSocket probe scripts and manually un-sil
          +encing it                                                     
      580 +mid-session. Now appends to `~/.zerodom/relay.log`. New tool  
      581 +`zerodom_status()` reports relay reachability, the active tab/
          +session state,                                                
      582 +and a live `Browser.getVersion` probe (bypassing `_page()`/Pla
          +ywright                                                       
      583 +entirely, bounded to a few seconds) when nothing's attached ye
          +t — the                                                       
      584 +built-in version of the exact manual check that used to take 9
          +0+ seconds of                                                 
      585 +`connect_over_cdp` hanging before anyone noticed the extension
          + handshake was                                                
      586 +stuck.                                                        
      587 +                                                              
      588 +**Deliberately not automated:** a full `--load-extension` real
          +-browser                                                      
      589 +integration test. `tests/test_extension_e2e.py` exists, opt-in
          + only                                                         
      590 +(`ZERODOM_E2E=1`), for the same reason `test_relay_server.py`'
          +s own docstring                                               
      591 +already gives — real Chrome + a real loaded extension is "the 
          +one thing this                                                
      592 +project correctly can't automate" reliably in a CI sandbox; th
          +e deterministic                                               
      593 +regression coverage lives in `test_relay.py` instead, at the `
          +BrowserModel`                                                 
      594 +level.                                                        
      595 +                                                              
      596 +**Would make this wrong:** `zerodom_eval_js`/`zerodom_get_cook
          +ies` being                                                    
      597 +exposed to a shared or multi-tenant `zerodom-mcp` deployment —
          + that's the                                                   
      598 +point at which "documented boundary" stops being enough and th
          +is needs an                                                   
      599 +actual access-control gate, not a comment.

      202  
      203 +⚠️ **`zerodom_eval_js` and `zerodom_get_cookies` are real powe
          +r, not a toy.** Both go through                               
      204 +the same `chrome.debugger` connection every other tool already
          + uses — no extra Chrome permission                            
      205 +is granted — but together they let whoever can call these tool
          +s read a user's live session                                  
      206 +cookies and run arbitrary code in their authenticated browser.
          + That's expected and useful for a                             
      207 +developer driving their own agent against their own browser (i
          +t's exactly what makes                                        
      208 +session-hijacking-style pentesting possible), and a real risk 
          +if `zerodom-mcp` is ever reachable                            
      209 +by an untrusted or prompt-injectable MCP client. Nothing here 
          +gates that — it's a documented                                
      210 +boundary, not an enforced one. See `docs/DECISIONS.md` D14.   
      211 +                                                              
      212  **An agent loop shouldn't re-read the page it already has.** T
           wo tools exist so it
      213  doesn't have to. `zerodom_find` answers "where's the dispatch 
           button?" with one
      214  line instead of the whole graph, and actions return a diff — `
           +` appeared, `-`

✻ Cogitated for 9m 11s

❯ and when the agent is surfing in a spefic tab in group that specific tab user

● Two distinct things here — let me handle them separately since they have very
  different technical shapes.

  On CSS/design reading — yes, already reachable, and worth a dedicated tool.
  zerodom_eval_js can already run getComputedStyle(el) today, but a
  purpose-built zerodom_get_styles(node_id) (computed styles + box model
  dimensions) is a cheap, clean addition mirroring zerodom_get_cookies.

  On the click-lock + cursor — there's a real technical trap here worth catching
  before writing code: a full-page overlay that blocks clicks would also block
  zerodom's own clicks, since chrome.debugger-dispatched input is real,
  hit-tested input — indistinguishable from a genuine user click at the DOM
  level. The fix is to keep the overlay locked at all times except the
  split-second zerodom's own action is actually running (unlock → act →
  re-lock), which also means zerodom_scroll's wheel dispatch needs the same
  treatment, or it'd get blocked by its own lock layer.

  Let me check the current highlight/log injection code before extending it:

  Read 1 file, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

      237 +# Real user clicks and chrome.debugger-dispatched clicks are i
          +ndistinguishable                                              
      238 +# at the DOM level — both are trusted input events. So this ca

          +n just flips                                                  
      243 +# pointer-events on every call.                               
      244 +_SET_LOCK_JS = """(locked) => {                               
      245 +  let layer = document.getElementById('zerodom-lock-layer');  
      246 +  if (!layer) {                                               
      247 +    layer = document.createElement('div');                    
      248 +    layer.id = 'zerodom-lock-layer';                          
      249 +    layer.style.cssText = 'position:fixed;inset:0;z-index:2147
          +483646;cursor:not-allowed;';                                  
      250 +    const swallow = (e) => { e.stopPropagation(); e.preventDef
          +ault(); };                                                    
      251 +    layer.addEventListener('click', swallow, true);           
      252 +    layer.addEventListener('mousedown', swallow, true);       
      253 +    layer.addEventListener('wheel', swallow, { capture: true, 
          +passive: false });                                            
      254 +    document.body.appendChild(layer);                         
      255 +                                                              
      256 +    const banner = document.createElement('div');

      275 +_MOVE_CURSOR_JS = """(el) => {                                
      276 +  const cursor = document.getElementById('zerodom-cursor');   
      277 +  if (!cursor) return;                                        
      278 +  const r = el.getBoundingClientRect();                       
      279 +  cursor.style.left = (r.left + r.width / 2) + 'px';

      284 +async def _show_driving(locator: Any) -> None:                
      285 +    """Ensures the lock layer/cursor exist and are locked, mov
          +es the visible                                                
      286 +    cursor to the element about to be acted on, then pulses th
          +e highlight —                                                 
      287 +    the full "an agent is driving this" visual, run before eve
          +ry real action

          +the real action.

      290 +    try:

      292 +        await page.evaluate(_SET_LOCK_JS, True)

      301 +    """The one moment real input is allowed through the lock l
          +ayer: while

          +y running.                                                    
      303 +    Real user clicks/scroll stay blocked every other moment. A

      311 +            pass                                              
      312 +    try:

● Update(docs/DECISIONS.md)
  ⎿  Added 51 lines
      597  exposed to a shared or multi-tenant `zerodom-mcp` deployment —
            that's the
      598  point at which "documented boundary" stops being enough and th
           is needs an
      599  actual access-control gate, not a comment.
      600 +                                                              
      601 +---                                                           
      602 +                                                              
      603 +## D15. A visible "zerodom is driving this tab" lock layer + c
          +ursor, and `zerodom_get_styles`                               
      604 +                                                              
      605 +**Decided:** 2026-09-19. Extends D14.                         
      606 +                                                              
      607 +**The ask:** while zerodom is acting on an attached (real-brow
          +ser) tab, the                                                 
      608 +real user shouldn't be able to click into it, and there should
          + be a visible                                                 
      609 +cursor the way Claude-in-Chrome shows one — not just the exist
          +ing 350ms                                                     
      610 +amber highlight pulse (D5-era), which only appears for a fract
          +ion of a                                                      
      611 +second around a single action.                                
      612 +                                                              
      613 +**The trap this would have shipped straight into:** a real use
          +r click and a                                                 
      614 +`chrome.debugger`-dispatched click are the same thing at the D
          +OM level —                                                    
      615 +both are trusted `Input.dispatchMouseEvent`-originated events.
          + There is no                                                  
      616 +JS-level way to tell them apart. A naive full-page `pointer-ev
          +ents`                                                         
      617 +click-blocking overlay would have blocked zerodom's *own* clic
          +ks exactly as                                                 
      618 +hard as the user's, since `locator.click()` ultimately dispatc
          +hes through the                                               
      619 +identical hit-tested input path a real click does.            
      620 +                                                              
      621 +**Fix:** the lock layer (`_SET_LOCK_JS`, `zerodom/mcp_server.p
          +y`) stays                                                     
      622 +locked (`pointer-events: auto`, swallows click/mousedown/wheel
          +) at every                                                    
      623 +moment *except* the split second zerodom's own action is actua
          +lly running —                                                 
      624 +toggled by `_unlocked_input()`, an async context manager wrapp
          +ing every real                                                
      625 +input dispatch: `_act()`'s click/fill/hover/press/upload, `_ac
          +t_drag()`'s                                                   
      626 +drag, and `zerodom_scroll()`'s wheel (all four dispatch real i
          +nput, all four                                                
      627 +needed the same unlock-around-the-call treatment, not just cli
          +ck/fill).                                                     
      628 +`_show_driving()` (renamed/extended from the old bare `_highli
          +ght()` call                                                   
      629 +site) also moves a visible fake cursor (`_MOVE_CURSOR_JS`) to 
          +the element                                                   
      630 +about to be acted on before every action, and a small banner (
          +"zerodom is                                                   
      631 +driving this tab") makes the lock legible instead of looking l
          +ike a broken                                                  
      632 +page. All of it — layer, banner, cursor — is created idempoten
          +tly on first                                                  
      633 +use and lives only in the DOM, so it disappears naturally on n
          +avigation like                                                
      634 +the existing highlight/log panel already do; nothing new to cl
          +ean up.                                                       
      635 +                                                              
      636 +**Not built:** an explicit "release control back to the user" 
          +tool. The lock                                                
      637 +is scoped to "zerodom is attached to this tab," matching the e
          +xisting                                                       
      638 +`attached` session concept exactly — there was no request for 
          +a middle state                                                
      639 +where zerodom holds the tab but isn't currently allowed to act
          + on it.                                                       
      640 +                                                              
      641 +**`zerodom_get_styles(node_id)`, same session:** computed styl
          +es (a curated                                                 
      642 +~20-property subset — layout, spacing, color, typography — not
      643 +`getComputedStyle()`'s full ~300-property dump) + box-model di
          +mensions for a                                                
      644 +node, for design/CSS review rather than interaction. Already r
          +eachable ad hoc                                               
      645 +via `zerodom_eval_js`; this is the purpose-built version.     
      646 +                                                              
      647 +**Would make this wrong:** a real product need for the user to
          + interject                                                    
      648 +mid-session without zerodom detaching first — that's the point
          + at which this                                                
      649 +needs an actual "pause"/"release" tool instead of the all-or-n
          +othing                                                        
      650 +attached-state lock.

      204 +**In an attached (real-browser) session, zerodom locks the tab

          +/scrolling from you are blocked the

      211  is granted — but together they let whoever can call these tool

● Done. Summary of this round:

  Click-lock + cursor — while zerodom is attached to a tab, a lock layer blocks
  real user clicks/scroll at all times except the split second zerodom's own
  action runs (_unlocked_input, wrapping every real input dispatch:
  click/fill/hover/press/upload/drag/scroll). A visible cursor moves to
  whatever's about to be acted on (_show_driving, extending the existing
  highlight-pulse mechanism), plus a small "zerodom is driving this tab" banner
  so it's legible, not broken-looking. The real trap here — that a real click
  and a chrome.debugger-dispatched click are indistinguishable trusted DOM
  events, so a naive always-on overlay would've blocked zerodom's own clicks too
  — is caught and documented in docs/DECISIONS.md D15.

  zerodom_get_styles(node_id) — computed styles (curated ~20-property set:
  layout, spacing, color, typography) + box-model dimensions, for design/CSS
  review specifically.

  Both covered by tests (201 passed, 1 cleanly skipped — the opt-in E2E), README
  tools table and D15 decision entry updated. Same as every round: restart
  Claude Code + reload the extension to actually see the lock/cursor live
  against your browser. Nothing committed yet.

      285 +async def _set_input_ignored(page: Any, ignored: bool) -> None
          +:                                                             
      286 +    """The actual "a real user can't click" block: Chrome's ow
          +n input                                                       
      287 +    pipeline (Input.setIgnoreInputEvents over a raw CDP sessio
          +n), not a                                                     
      288 +    page-injected DOM trick — enforced by the browser before t
          +he page's own                                                 
      289 +    JS or any content it controls ever sees the event. Real, O
          +S-originated                                                  
      290 +    input is what this flag exists to ignore; chrome.debugger-
          +dispatched                                                    
      291 +    input (what zerodom's own actions use) is unaffected by it
          +, which is the                                                
      292 +    documented purpose of this specific CDP method — but it's 
          +still toggled                                                 
      293 +    around each action in _unlocked_input rather than set once
          + and left on,                                                 
      294 +    as a defensive measure in case that isn't as absolute as d
          +ocumented.                                                    
      295 +    Cosmetic-adjacent: a failure here must never block the rea
          +l action.                                                     
      296 +    """
